I just haven’t felt that interested in it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried about your eating habits?\n\nPatient: A little, I guess. My jeans feeling loose is what made me think maybe I should mention it.\n\nDoctor: How much weight do you think you've lost since you started skipping meals?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure exactly. I haven’t weighed myself. I just noticed over the last few weeks I’ve been missing meals and my jeans feel looser, so… maybe some, but I couldn’t give a number.\n\nDoctor: Is the lack of interest in eating due to not feeling hungry or is it because you're just not interested in food itself?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more that I’m just not really interested in food. My jeans feel a little loose now, so… I figured I should bring it up.\n\nDoctor: Have you been trying to lose weight?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I haven’t been trying to. I just… haven’t had much appetite, I guess. I skip meals without thinking, and then dinner doesn’t really sound good either. My jeans being loose is what made me notice.\n\nDoctor: Have you been happy about the weight loss?\n\nPatient: No… not happy, exactly. It’s more that I noticed my jeans getting loose and realized I’d been skipping meals and barely eating dinner. That’s why I brought it up.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, I haven’t really been worrying about gaining weight. I just noticed I wasn’t eating much.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be upset if you gained back the weight you lost?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d be upset, no. It’s not really about the weight for me. I just noticed that for a few weeks I’ve been skipping breakfast and lunch without meaning to, and then kind of picking at dinner… even when my wife cooks. Then my jeans started feeling loose, so… that’s what made me mention it.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of eating?\n\nPatient: No… I wouldn’t say I’m afraid of eating. I just haven’t felt that interested in it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried about your eating habits?\n\nPatient: A little, I guess. Not worried like… afraid of food or anything. It’s more that I noticed I keep skipping breakfast and lunch without really meaning to, and then I barely eat dinner. My jeans feeling loose is what made me think maybe I should mention it.\n\nDoctor: How much weight do you think you've lost since you started skipping meals?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure exactly. I haven’t weighed myself. I just noticed over the last few weeks I’ve been missing meals and my jeans feel looser, so… maybe some, but I couldn’t give a number.\n\nDoctor: Is the lack of interest in eating due to not feeling hungry or is it because you're just not interested in food itself?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more that I’m just not really interested in food. Like, I’ll skip breakfast and lunch without really meaning to, and then at dinner I kind of just pick at it, even if my wife cooked. That’s what made me notice it… and my jeans being loose.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad or depressed when you think about eating or when you're supposed to eat?\n\nPatient: Not really about eating specifically. It’s more… most days lately I wake up feeling kind of heavy and flat. The apartment feels too quiet, and even picking up my guitar doesn’t really do much. I haven’t checked. I just know the last few weeks I’ve been skipping breakfast and lunch, not eating much dinner, and my jeans feel looser.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of interest in food is related to the heavy and flat feeling you wake up with most days?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s like I wake up feeling heavy and kind of flat most days, and then food just doesn’t really register. My jeans are getting a little loose, so… I guess if my mood lifted, maybe food would seem more normal again.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of interest in food would improve if your mood were better?\n\nPatient: Yeah… probably. I mean, I think it’s tied to that. It’s just been these past few weeks where I don’t really eat much—skip breakfast and lunch, then barely touch dinner. If I felt better, maybe I’d care about food again.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of interest in food is more because of not feeling like yourself or because food itself doesn't appeal to you?\n\nPatient: More not feeling like myself, I think. Food doesn’t exactly gross me out or anything… I just don’t think about it. I’ll miss breakfast and lunch, then at dinner I only pick at it, even if my wife made something. It’s been a few weeks like that. My jeans are a little loose now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
